The rules are simple:
-Post a quote
-You can post quotes only if you have guessed the last quote correctly
-The quote should be commonly known
-You must know from where the quote you posted is from
-The quote must be from a TV show/movie, A book, or someone has said it
-If a quote isn't guessed in 2 days, the first to post will start.

Thank you. The first one to post may start.

#42353 10 2004-12-25T10:29:52Z JeR(K)
I've been watching your game for a while and I've been writing this for a while, too. Any chance I could join with this character? Just read the bio before judging, okay?

BIO

Name:Sha-Eth (The Different One) Friends call him just Sha.
Race:Szarzina
Class:Archer
Gender:Male
Appearance:A bit skinny for a Szarzina, shady person
Equipment:Constantly wears: Dark robe and hood. Keeps his bow and knife always handy.

Sha-Eth grew up in Arein (his name wasn't Sha-Eth back then, it was changed later. He has never told anyone what his real name was.) as the son of a very highly esteemed Lieutenant of the Royal Army. His father trained him in all kinds of martial arts when Sha was still very young. Sha was a peace-loving person that had various disagreements with his family and the whole city, that caused him to be banished in the age of 17, as the city leaders decided that Sha was a threat to the society.

Sha-Eth then lived 3 years in the open desert building himself a boat, so he knows a lot about survival in the desert. His hatred on his people grew during those years and finally, after the 3 long years, Sha managed to finish his boat and sailed off to Aeken, to human civilization.

While on human land, Sha had to hide his face, which is the reason he always wears a long robe and keeps his hood up. Sha also had trained his accent in the desert so he wouldn't say the Sssss in every word, but he still can't control that accent when he's angry. Sha couldn't get normal work the way he was, so he started a "career" as a mercenary/thief. He was very considerate about his targets, as he didn't want to kill anyone who was going to the war etc. -and fight the Nagas and the other Szarzina. Sha had been wandering in the city one day and he discovered an inn with a sign: "The Shivering Norseman, No Questions Asked" This seemed like his kind of place. He took a room from that inn and made himself at home. Sha's roommate that he made friends with, was quite unlikely for a friend; he was a dwarf. Sha-Eth and the dwarf who said his name was Gistil, became good friends. Gistil was in Aeken on a business trip for his family and that he lived in Forr. Gistil had heard that the reptilians had attacked Forr but Gistil didn't have the money to get back there. Sha offered to pay the both's trips as he was heading that way, too. The friends packed up and quikcly left Aeken. The pair of them had heard some rumours about a group that was fighting the lizardmen and that that group was in Forr just now. Sha and Gistil arrived in Forr after a rather short boat-trip, and that is where Sha's story (hopefully) picks up.




Overall:

Strengths:
*Long-Range weapons
*Desert Survival
*Martial Arts
*Truly hates the reptilian style butchering

Weaknesses:
*Eyes have been in the darkness of his robe for too long, so they can't stand the sun.
*Accent
*Lizard (prejudices)


*wow* That was my longest post ever, I think.

Sha-Eth
~~~

Sha was wandering in the streets of Kurzano, pondering about his fate. He had een rescued by a band of men and dwarves. He oweb his life to these men. And they had abandoned him, told him to look for a better place to live. Suddenly the headlines of a newspaper in a stand (hey, this is an rp, it's not ACTUALLY medieval...) made his eyes go wide open. He quickly bought one copy of the paper. No, this isn't true...
He felt his mind flash back to the time he was a kid.

"Son, do you see that building over there?" "Yup. That is the place you work in, right?", Sha answered. "Yes. But you must know one thing about it. You might not understand this, but you will one day. That building is the head of scientific research (meaning magic in medieval times) in the whole world. We are creating fearsome ways of ending war more efficiently there. You mustn't go in there under any circumstances. Do you understand?" "Yes I do, father."
One day, a couple of months later, he had seen that building burn down. And so had done his father. There had been a lot of people wandering in the ruins the same day, as if looking for something.

Sha used his last money to buy himself a horse and some food. And thus, he rode north.

BTW, b'zugda-hiara = A killing insult in Dwarfish. It means 'Lawn ornament' But it can be used as a term for endearment, too.

#62755 3 2005-04-29T13:15:31Z JeR(K)
Btw, there was no such map on the internet.

Okay, my character is named Darren, and he is a ranger. So he's a ranged fighter, and knows a lot of survival in the wilderness. He had a magi friend called Jake on the surface who taught him a few of the simplest spells (like: Heal, Light, Cure, the first three spells). He rarely visited the town of *town* near the forest he guarded and which he lived in. When he was in *town*, Darren often helped the villagers with their problems, sometimes along with his friend Jake. Although *town* was a very distant one, far away from everything. But one day, the empire came there. At first they seemed like just a good king's troops, but they had no respect whatsoever for the people of the village and the unique flora and fauna of *town*. Once the troops set foot in Darren's forest, hacking down trees and killing animals for fun, Darren was furious. But he still didn't do anything because he had heard of innocent people being banished to Avernum, the underworld. But once Darren heard that someone had destroyed the water supplies of the troops, he couldn't hold the laughter. After a nice evening in *town* with his friends, Darren returned home, to his cottage in the forest. There, three men wearing heavy armor bearing the emperor's symbol, the sun (wasn't it?), were waiting for him. They accused him of the sabotage that had been done. Darren pleaded his innocence (<-- is that correctly spelled and stuff?) and said that no harm had been done, as wells were everywhere in this region. But the soldiers had to get someone guilty for this crime and they had no other suspects. So Darren was doomed for Avernum. He got the chance, however, to say goodbye to his friends, and he got a goodbyegift from Jake, a ball that Jake said would allow Darren to communicate with the owner of the counterpart, that was in the possession of Jake, of course. It would need a special crystal, mined from Avernum to work, though, and Jake only had one. After a brief moment with his dearest friends, Darren left for *whatever is the place where they send the people down to Avernum* and soon, too soon, Darren thought, he was in the dim green light of Avernum, with some other new arrivals, as they were called.

Wow, how did I manage to write all that?

Darren woke up in a dimly lit room. He had seen a dream that he was dumped into Avernum ahead of schedule when he was asleep. Wait a minute. That wasn't a dream. Darren sat on a crude bed made of bamboo-like material. The door of the room opened, giving more light to the room. It was made of stone. There was a backpack on thew floor. After the rather old man had told Darren the general stuff about Avernum, he gave Darren a scroll containing a bit more in-depth info. Then Darren grabbed his bag and investigated it. He found some food and a stone knife and a rather poor leather jacket. He went outside of the town, not far, to make himself a bow and a few arrows. When he came back, he was told that another newcomer had arrived to Avernum, and went to find out who he was.

#87068 3 2005-07-30T09:51:50Z JeR(K)
I prefer St. Petersburg over Moscow. Moscow's just too big and it's the target of all kinds of terrorist attacks and stuff. Petersburg has lots of culture, (Catharine palace, Hermitage, etc.) and if you don't want russian food, just go round the corner and you'll be sure to find a Subway, McDonalds, Pizza Hut or something else. Also if you're looking for Computer Software/Movies, 505.ru is a GREAT store with lots of cheap games, other software and music and movies. (i won't question their legality, they've worked well so far).

Also, the subway (not the fastfood, the underground railway) is one of the deepest in the world. You go like 125 meters on the moving stairs (whaddya call it, an escalator?).

And they sell coca-cola in 4L packages (12 x 0.33L cans).

#87495 10 2005-08-01T16:31:13Z JeR(K)
WOW. I just ate the most peculiar food. Why don't they make that in finland? The meal was called Krotshki-Kartotshki, which stands for Fried Potato (or something like that). Well, as most of you think, how can a fried potato be peculiar? Well, they mashed the inside of the potato, then put a 5cmx5cmx5cm cube of butter in it, then some onion, mushrooms and meat (there was a whole lot of other ingredients you could choose to put into the potato, but I wasn't in THAT much experimental mood). Anyways, it tasted like nothing I've ever tasted before, but it was actually good!

It must be like a russian equivalent of hamburger.

#90114 2 2005-08-12T07:02:25Z JeR(K)
Ok I'll give you a quick lesson on the language learning system in finland.

Elementary (6 years long):

-English starting from the first year. (English is the "A1"-language)
-Fourth grade you have an optional language, options vary in schools, you don't *HAVE* to take anything ("A2")

High School (3/4 years long)

-English continues through high school
-New "B1" language: Swedish, throughout high school
-"A2" continues
-At the first year, you choose some subjects (we got to choose 2 or 3, 2 if you had A2, the maximum of choises is normally 12 subjects (if you take an hour subjs.), headmaster doesn't want to get all the timetables straight, takes too much of her precious time... )
-If you take a language as a subject, it will be "B2"



My languages:

A1: English

A2: German

B1: Swedish

B2: -

I'll prolly take another language in college if I get there. (it's not the college you have, it's a whole different system here). Could be latin.

#169812 5 2006-04-30T09:35:55Z JeR(K)
ZZ9, that's a weird coincidence. I just recently downloaded Dofus. Got bored with it though.

Anarchy Online (AO for short) is a previously pay-to-play-MMORPG, but now it's completely free to download and play. The add-ons still cost you though.

Also, Guild Wars is something I like to call a "Massively Multiplayer Single Player Role Playing Game", because it has no monthly fees, and plays very differently as opposed to, say, WoW or AO. They just released their first stand-alone add-on, too! The two games are separate, you don't need the other to play the other better or anything! This is a really good game.
Although if you are a fan of grinding and farming and leveling, this game is not about those things. It's about teamwork and strategy, much more than WoW etc. are.

#12971 1 2006-12-18T17:59:40Z JeR(K)
Hey, everybody. It's been a while.

I suppose half of you are asking yourselves right now, "Who is this guy?". I'm the over-enthusiastic nationalist guy from Finland. Back in the day I worked together on a lot of stuff with my friend, who you know as Igi, or Igelos. If I remember correctly. I see a lot of new faces around nowadays. But the old loyal guys are still here. Ol' Blizz. Suki. And all the others I can't think of right now.
Like you've all come to expect from me in the past, this post was meant to be written around June or July. And the picture I'm posting below was meant to be posted since 7th grade (two years ago), but that was because I lacked the proper equipment. Like a camera. Heh.

So, what have I been doing lately? My life's been going on at school steadily, I'll continue to the upper secondary (senior high) after this year, study enough languages so I can work as a translator in the future. I've actually started translating a bit, starting a month or two ago, at divxfinland.org, a finnish subtitling community. And yeah, I've still been playing N from time to time, when I'm bored. It's a game of memories to me nowadays. Good memories. A thank-you to this community and everyone in it at this point would be proper, no less Mare and Raigan themselves. I'm pretty sure I'll start spending some time here again. Probably not even on a daily basis, but I'll probably be here anyways.

Then, onto this weird picture I took. Tell me what you see in it. It took Sucker a while to realise what I think it looks like when I showed this to him sometime in an IM conversation.
It's a christian (as far as I know) icon, of which there is a picture in my religious education class at my school:


Hint: See a familiar shape? Does it, maybe, induce a sense of running a way and jumping a couple meters in the air, or maybe hiding in a curved surface?

I'm pretty sure that'd be all I had to say. Oh yeah, and if I'm nowhere to be found and you have something important to say, one place I can be found in often would be Heroes of Ivalice. That's the place in Kablizzy's signature. Lovin' it so far.

And one other thing. Have a huge imaginary cookie if you can tell me where the topic Title (not the description) is from. And that'd be without the (s) part.

Any questions, miscellaneous comments or gatherings of huge mobs for any of my unfinished projects are welcome.

I've played many of them. Won't bother to list all. FFVI, FFVII, FFVIII and FFT being the ones I liked most. That is, if FFVI is the SNES one with the best class system ever. On the subject of FFTA, well, it was blasphemy as I see it. In that area I'm on the same lines as Kablizzy. Also proved by the point we both have experience playing Heroes of Ivalice

And FFVIII was the first real FF I ever played (I played FFIII Legends on the Game Boy when I was a kid.). VIII is the best of the, you know, Vanilla Final Fantasies, if you know what I mean. The plot was awesome, etc. I have the Soundtrack of FFVIII on my mp3 player. I got it after someone played me a song that belonged to it. I've never felt such nostalgia before. It not only reminds me of the game, but a few ex-classmates that played it through at the same time...

But overrated? Maybe a little, in the way that some of the games (FFTA, FF Legends-series) were disappointments. But there are far more overrated series out there.
